      +Chonglin Zhan Hi friend, 
      
      James has very clear pronunciation, which makes it easy to understand him. Unfortunately, many other english speakers do not have such clear pronunciation; they might not pronounce clearly some syllables of a word, for example. They also might speak a little faster. Another reason you might have some difficulty is that James has a particular accent and you might have gotten used to hearing that accent in english. This can make it hard to understand other english speakers with different accents. I wouldn't worry too much! With time, it's definitely something you can get used to.

## Learn English Grammar: Modals - "could" or "should"? — ENGLISH with James · engVid
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kLIQNJGwr44
- Video ID: kLIQNJGwr44
- Views: 512 199 · comments total: 224 · engagement: 0.44 comments/1k views
- published 2014-10-01 · new comments in this batch: 217

**@diego4493115** (👍80 · 2015-08-12) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kLIQNJGwr44&lc=Ugg5SeUai33qrXgCoAEC
  Best teacher I've ever seen. Thank you!
    ↳ **@pmAdministrator** (👍0 · 2018-08-12) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kLIQNJGwr44&lc=Ugg5SeUai33qrXgCoAEC.7-H0Z7-JKjo8Wo7IMJ3qnM
      Best teacher that YOU'VE EVER SEEN?
      That seem to have something to do with his looks.
      Women...

**@fr6836-x1i** (👍3 · 2021-08-12) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kLIQNJGwr44&lc=Ugx3JhoJZI_sKTYH5vx4AaABAg
  Could is used for 
  1)past capability- i could run 20 miles when i was young
  2) request/permission (may and can are bad for this) - could you suggest a good brand of phone?
  3) possibility (you can also use may/might) : he could/may/might make a full recovery one day
  
  
  Should is used generally for giving suggestions. However, would can also be used
  1)you SHOULD drink 3 litres of water everyday
  2) I WOULD suggest drinking 3 litres of water everyday

  Wow! What great tips! I'm not an ESL learner, but I definitely learned a lot from this video. I live in Korea, and sometimes I meet people who end up asking for my contact information. I didn't want to give it to them, because I never saw us being friends or I wasn't interested in them, but I gave it to them anyway.
  
  So from this video, I learned to say something like, "Oh! How nice of you to ask for my kakao (messenger), but you probably have a lot of people on there and don't have room for me." Or Facebook, "...you probably have so many friends already, you don't need me on there."
  
  Thanks for the video :)

**@paulinho543** (👍5 · 2014-08-12) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=alg7qHta0Sk&lc=UggRDXde1QYl93gCoAEC
  To my mind, the right thing to do is not to think if the word is large or short. If the word ends with a sequence of "consonant", "vowel" and "consonant" (CVC pattern), we have to double the last consonant and add ING or ED in the end. For example:
  
  Rub (Consonant/vowel/Consonant) = rubbed
  Sit (Consonant/vowel/Consonant) = Sitting
  Stop (Consonant/vowel/Consonant) = Stopping
  
  But, like everything in life, there are exceptions. For instance, in words with two syllables if the stress is on the first syllable, we do not have to double the last consonant, even if the word ends with a CVC pattern. For example:
  
  Visit (CVC pattern, but the stress is on the first syllable 'vis') = visited
  Enter (CVC pattern, but the stress is on the first syllable 'en') = entering
  
  Simple as that.
  
  And yes, we Brazilians have problems with the pronunciation of certain words that do not have a vowel in the end or the sound of it is silent.

    ↳ **@raphaelahwang7602** (👍1 · 2015-08-12) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=P1GAGSdAvxM&lc=Ugj7qxdL5RDW3XgCoAEC.7-H0Z7-49-y7-dJjZTsUFe
      But see, he mentioned how you put a word (here money) in the middle, which you associate with other things (here love, hard work, time). You write those down around the word money and connect it with branches. What do you associate with love? Hearts, marriage, dates? Write those things down and connect it with branches. You can go on like this forever. Do this in a language you know and put the word in your language below the foreign word you want to memorize!! I hope this helped. :)
